Accuracy by market: each live model vs baseline vs odds-implied

baseline_v0

ModelBaselineOdds-implied
  • 1X2: beats baseline, loses to odds
  • BTTS: beats baseline, beats odds
  • O/U 2.5: beats baseline, loses to odds
  • O/U 1.5: loses to baseline, loses to odds
  • Correct Score: loses to baseline, loses to odds
  • HT 1X2: beats baseline, loses to odds
  • HT O/U 1.5: loses to baseline, beats odds
  • HT O/U 0.5: ties baseline, loses to odds

Paper betslips: 6 slips, ₦3,000 every round (1X2 top 5 ₦500, HT O/U 0.5 top 5 ₦500, BTTS top 5 ₦500, 1X2 top 3 ₦500, HT O/U 0.5 top 3 ₦500, BTTS top 3 ₦500)

Simulated accumulators -- no real bets are placed. Every round gets one slip per market (the 5 games with the strongest pick in that market) plus one slip with every game's single strongest pick across all six markets. "Chance" = the combined estimate used to pick: the average of the model, Bet9ja's margin-free odds, and how often similar past picks won (learned only from earlier rounds). A slip pays stake × all odds multiplied, and only if every pick wins. Back-tested over every round since tracking began, so the totals include history from before this section existed.

Cut tracker: are "sure" picks losing more than the odds say?

Checks the suspicion that the bookmaker makes low-odds favourites lose more often than their prices imply. Every number is compared with what Bet9ja's own odds predicted (margin removed). ✅ = within normal luck; ⚠️ = a gap bigger than luck usually explains (|z| ≥ 2) -- only meaningful if it keeps showing up. Includes tickets losing by exactly one pick, the most painful kind of loss.

Season 32: ✅ normal -- results match the odds · all seasons: ✅ normal -- results match the odds

CheckSeason 32All seasons
Favourites won (1X2, every game)✅ 55.7% vs 54.8% expected (n=318)✅ 54.5% vs 54.6% expected (n=7,961)
Our ticket picks won✅ 62.6% vs 65.6% expected (n=768)✅ 63.5% vs 63.7% expected (n=11,766)
Tickets won✅ 34 vs 43 expected (n=192)✅ 308 vs 308 expected (n=2,384)
Tickets lost by exactly 1 pick✅ 75 vs 70 expected (n=192)✅ 713 vs 703 expected (n=2,384)

Return on a flat 1-unit stake, per market

What accuracy can't show: whether backing every pick at its closing odds would have made money. Bet9ja's Zoom odds are very well calibrated (outcomes priced at 25% happen ~25% of the time), so every strategy trends toward minus the bookmaker's margin (~3-8% depending on market). A model with a real edge would show up here as a positive ROI that holds over thousands of bets. "Odds fav" = always backing the bookmaker's favourite. Correct Score's history includes months of "Other" picks from a since-fixed bug, which almost never won.

Reading this dashboard: since 2026-10-11 only baseline_v0 runs live -- ml_v1 and blend_v1 were retired after ~20k predictions showed neither beating the odds (see the return and log-loss tables); their history stays above. Original notes: three models ran live in parallel and were tracked independently. baseline_v0 is a placeholder using bookmaker odds where scraped (1X2, BTTS, O/U 2.5, O/U 1.5, HT 1X2, HT O/U 1.5/0.5) and a Poisson goal-expectation model elsewhere (Correct Score) -- not a trained model. ml_v1 is a real logistic-regression (1X2, BTTS) + Poisson-regression (goals) model, trained only on team form/H2H/season-rate/cross-season-strength stats -- deliberately NOT on odds, so "beats odds" stays a meaningful comparison rather than a circular one. A 5-fold cross-validated backtest (2,300+ historical matches) shows ml_v1 beating dumb baselines on 1X2, CorrectScore, and HT_1X2, still losing on OU2.5 -- it is not yet clearly better than the placeholder overall, hence running both live rather than replacing one with the other. "Baseline" (the reference column, not baseline_v0 the model) is a dumb non-model reference: always-Home for 1X2, most-common outcome so far for everything else. "Odds-implied" now applies to every market, including the 1st-half markets (a separate page/tab on bet9ja, easy to miss -- see scraper/fixtures_scraper.py) and CorrectScore (reads straight off the scraped full grid). Small sample sizes make any of these percentages noisy; treat them as directional until many more rounds have been reconciled.

Round numbers on the "upcoming" panel of the round browser are the pipeline's own best-guess inference (the live round read straight off the site, plus one -- see scraper/results_scraper.py), made against a fixtures page that carries no round label at all -- it can still occasionally be off. Use the ←/→ buttons above to step through recent history; reconciliation itself does not depend on this number (matches are found by team pairing, not round number), so accuracy figures are unaffected either way.
